- `smoke/soak-mini` with `sweep=20`: 10 Pass, 10 Known (all CORE-011). The first sweep also flagged a fighter left in a dying goblin's square when a grapple ended; that is legal next to a helpless creature (PHB p.148), so the `grid` invariant now allows it, and the second sweep had no other violation.
- Ad-hoc definitions built inline: a Stats fighter with a forced first d20 and a forced initiative order (Pass), a Ui fighter whose turns the harness ended (Pass), and an abort mid-fight (Halted, Inconclusive, run ABORTED) followed by a clean second Start. After static suites in the same Play session, Start answered `needsFresh`.
- Speed: about 70 frames per second at the start of a Play session, falling to about 9 by the end of a session of about 60 fights, with the editor focused on every frame. The scene then held 52,931 `PooledLogMsg` objects (UI-001, fixed 2026-10-08; section 3.5). Long batches still belong in fresh Play sessions (`QueueFresh` with `perSession`).
- After the review fixes (guarded run loop, interrupted state, Error Pause handling, CORE-011 and CORE-037 cause split), in a fresh Play session: `Start("smoke/*", "1-3", "repeat=2")` again gave 18 of 18 Pass, run OK, every repetition the same hash; a static-suite pass started mid-run was refused; `sweep=12` over smoke/two-goblins-ai and smoke/soak-mini gave 10 Pass and 14 Known, every Known a victory-side `combat-end-detected` with `cause=hp<=0` waived by CORE-011; exiting Play mode mid-run left status.json `interrupted` and a summary with verdict INTERRUPTED, all 10 jobs in `notRun` and a resume filter.
- Timeout was exercised later by the soak (run 20261007-202222, seed 27, before the log-leak sweep: 157 frames in 600 s; the diag named the phase, the current actor and the open panels). Not exercised: the SoftLock (and its diagnosis), SkipChainRunaway, Paused, Contaminated-mid-job and Exception endings, a runner-phase exception, a recompile during Play, a Console Error Pause, the Deflect Arrows and bull rush or overrun econ adjustments, and frame advance with the editor unfocused.

### 3.5 Fresh-session batches and the AI-vs-AI soak

**Fresh-session batches** (`ScenarioBatchDriver.cs`, `[InitializeOnLoad]`). From edit mode, `Tests.Scenarios.ScenarioHarness.QueueFresh(filters, seeds, options)` queues one fresh Play session per comma-separated filter (seeds and options as for `Start`, applied to each) and enters Play mode. The option `perSession=N` is the driver's own: it also splits the seeds into sessions of N seeds (it cannot be combined with `sweep`). `keep` is refused (the driver leaves Play mode as soon as a run ends), and so is a project whose Enter Play Mode Options turn off the domain reload (the sessions would not be fresh). The queue (at most 50 entries) lives in `SessionState`, so it survives the domain reload of every Play-mode entry. A second `QueueFresh` in the same CommandScript appends to the same batch; once Play mode has started it is refused, and once the batch is idle it starts a new batch and drops the old results. In each session the driver waits for a ready GameManager (60 s at most), calls `Start`, and when the run ends (an Error outcome or an Abort ends it too) records the run id, verdict and summary path, pops the entry and exits Play mode; back in edit mode it enters Play mode again for the next entry. Leaving Play mode in the middle of a run (by hand or a crash) has the runner write its summary and soak rows at once, records that entry as INTERRUPTED and drops it; if entries remain, the batch pauses until `ScenarioHarness.ResumeQueue()` (or `ClearQueue()`), otherwise the batch's soak report is merged. A script recompile during a run (Recompile And Continue Playing) kills the run in a domain reload; after the reload the driver records the entry as INTERRUPTED in the same way and exits Play mode. A recompile on leaving Play mode (Recompile After Finished Playing) drops the pending Play-mode entry; after that reload the driver enters Play mode again for a queued, unpaused batch. `ClearQueue()` empties the queue and, with no run going, merges the batch's soak report.

Progress: `ScenarioHarness.Poll()` (field `batch`: the full state while entries are queued or running, afterwards a compact one with the batch id, the result and verdict counts, `warnings` (the sum of the runs' summary warnings, when any) and the report paths) or `Logs/Scenarios/batch.json` (state `queued`, `running`, `paused` or `idle`, the queue, `currentRunId`, `currentEntry`, `currentStatusPath` and the results; each result has `warnings`, its run's summary warning count, when there are any). batch.json is written only on changes, so its `heartbeatUtc` stands still during a long session; liveness comes from `heartbeatUtc` in the file at `currentStatusPath` (the run's status.json, written about once a second while a job runs): older than about 15 s while batch.json says `running` means the session has stalled or the editor is gone. A background loop can watch batch.json without the MCP; use an absolute path, because agent shells reset the working directory: `B=F:/DNDPrototype/Logs/Scenarios/batch.json; for i in $(seq 1 200); do grep -qE '"state":"(idle|paused)"' $B && break; sleep 3; done; grep -oE '"state":"[a-z]+"' $B | head -1`. The loop stops after 10 minutes without saying so: read the state it prints and start it again while that is `queued` or `running` (one job can take up to its wall cap, 600 s for the soak). A fresh session costs about 7 s of entering and leaving Play mode. On 2026-10-09 Play mode stalled on entry, with the editor counted as focused or not (the player loop held at frame 2 to 7 for about 80 s or more), and an entry that no MCP call reached during the stall failed with "GameManager not ready 60 s" (TST-036; section 3.3). Later that day both entries of a batch failed that way although polls reached the editor, after Play-mode reloads of about 175 s (TST-037): check the reload time in `Editor.log` first (section 3.1, notes). After the editor restart, the batch 8 batches of the same evening (reloads of about 3.2 s) had no stall in any of their seven sessions. `QueueFresh` is called from edit mode and the driver enters and leaves Play mode itself, so a batch does not depend on any MCP call made in Play mode; a failed call in Play mode does not block starting one, and its progress can be read from batch.json on disk. While TST-036 is open: (1) after `QueueFresh`, call `ScenarioHarness.Poll()` through the MCP about every 20 to 30 s for as long as batch.json says `queued` or `running` (the bash loop above only reads the file and never reaches the editor; in batch 7 every entry polled like this passed, though it is not proven that the poll is what freed the stall). (2) A result whose `error` is "GameManager not ready 60 s after entering Play mode" ran no job and wrote no trace, so it is safe to retry: note the batch's run ids (a new batch drops the old results), queue the same filter and seeds again with `QueueFresh` once the batch is idle, and for a soak merge the runs of both batches with `ScenarioHarness.SoakReport("runId1,runId2,...")`, as batch 7 did. Never edit scripts while a batch runs.

Verified 2026-10-07 (Unity 6000.4.0f1 through the MCP, editor not focused): `QueueFresh("rules/pin-release*,rules/maneuver-trip", "1,2", "")` ran two fresh sessions (runs 20261007-194641: 2 Pass, 2 XFail CMB-122; 20261007-194650: 2 Pass), each about 8 s from entering Play mode to its result, both summaries written and the editor back in edit mode with no hook left set. Exiting Play mode by hand during a run gave an INTERRUPTED result, and with nothing left in the queue no pause. After the review fixes (same day): a two-entry batch (`soak/*` seeds 1-6, then `rules/maneuver-trip`) exited by hand during job 3 of run 20261007-210928 recorded INTERRUPTED with the run's summary and soak rows already written, and paused; `ClearQueue()` then merged those 2 finished jobs into `soak-20261007-210920.json`. `QueueFresh` with `keep` was refused, and the two-session check above ran again OK (runs 20261007-211002 and -211012) with the polling loop shown above. The domain-reload reconciliation was not exercised (it needs a recompile during a batch).

**Cast-stage side channel and trace diff** (batch 11 step 2, 2026-10-15; [designs/cast_core_batch11.md](designs/cast_core_batch11.md)). The cast pipelines mark every stage of a cast through `CastStageTrace.Mark` (`Spell/Casting/Core/CastStageTrace.cs`), which raises the inert `ScenarioHooks.CastStage`: begin (the pipeline's method name), legality, cost, distraction (entangled, grappled), failure (ASF, Blink), spend, concentration on cast, provocation, counterspell, Ring of Counterspells, invisibility break, Mirror Image, target Blink, globe, `SpellCaster.Cast`, the handler that claims the cast (its method name; a handler marks only once it is past every check that can still decline the cast, so a missed Ray of Enfeeblement or Touch of Idiocy writes no `handler` line; the Sound Burst stun and the Shield Other link act and then let the generic path go on, marked `<name> (continues)`; Summon Monster and Summon Swarm mark `PerformSummonMonsterCast` or `PerformSummonSwarmCast` once the provocation lets the cast through), buff and combat end (the NPC path's combat end is marked in `TryNPCPerformSpellCastForAI`, its only `EvaluateCombatEnd` site) (raise sites: [architecture/combat-and-grid.md](architecture/combat-and-grid.md#scenario-hooks-test-instrumentation)). Outside the harness the hook is null and a mark does nothing: no log, no dice, no allocation (constant strings, or a detail built only behind `ScenarioHooks.CastStage != null`). While a job runs, the trace subscribes and keeps one JSON line per stage: `i`, `afterSeq` (the seq of the last trace event before the stage), `r`, `t`, `by` (the actor key, or `name:<name>` for an actor the trace has not keyed: the handler never registers an actor), `spell`, `stage`, `detail`. They are written to `Logs/Scenarios/<runId>/<jobId>/caststages.jsonl` when the job is finalized, only for jobs that marked a stage; the file is not part of the trace or its hash, so every trace hash is the same with or without it (checked on the reference run below).

To explain a changed trace hash, compare the two runs of the job: `Tests.Scenarios.ScenarioTraceDiff.FirstDivergence(runA, runB, jobId, ignoreEvents)` (editor only, edit or Play mode, reads files only; a run id or a run directory path; `jobId` is the trace file name, e.g. `rules_spell-save-dc__s1`, `.jsonl` optional). It compares the hashed form of the two traces (no `f`, wall-clock or host fields, no `watchdog` or `log-leak` events) event by event and returns compact JSON: `identical`, the compared event counts, and for a divergence its `index`, `seqA`/`seqB`, the two events `a` and `b` (null where one trace ended), the 4 events before it in each run (`contextA`, `contextB`), and the cast stages of each run marked from the first context event to the diverging event (`castStagesA`, `castStagesB`). `ignoreEvents` drops events by their `ev` name from both traces first (for example `new[] { "cast" }` when a change adds `cast` events for Ui casters); seq numbers and the fields that cite them (`attackSeq`, `seqs`) are then left out of the comparison. Procedure: run the job's filter and seeds in a fresh session (`QueueFresh`), read both runs' `summary.json`, and for each job whose `hash` differs call `FirstDivergence` from a CommandScript (`result.Log(Tests.Scenarios.ScenarioTraceDiff.FirstDivergence("20261015-014821", "<newRun>", "rules_spell-save-dc__s1", new string[0]));`); a pure-move step expects `identical`, a rules fix expects the first divergence at the rule it changed, and the cast-stage lines say which stage of which pipeline it was. A run of batch 10 or earlier has no `caststages.jsonl`, so its `castStages` list is empty.

**Spell routing golden file.** `Tests.Magic.SpellRoutingGoldenTests` (static suite, edit or Play mode) records for every SpellDatabase spell, from data and without casting: the `EffectType` after `SpellCategoryClassifier.ReclassifyAll`, `TargetType`, `AoEShapeType`, `SpellEffectRouting.ReachesApplySpellBuff`, the branch `BeginPendingSpellTargeting` takes for a PC cast (`aoe`, `summon`, `swarm`, `wish`, `self`, `single`, or `grease` for Grease, whose branch is the caster's mode choice) and whether a single-target request refuses it on data (an area-routed spell, `AoESystem.RoutesToArea`: "area-needs-aim" for every caster since batch 11 step 25, which replaced the NPC-only refusal of AI-001; the column keeps its name `npcRefuses`; the step's review added Prayer, the one Self spell with a burst, so 63 rows are true). It compares them with `tools/tests/spell-routing-golden.json` (one spell per line, ordinal id order; 292 spells on 2026-10-15: 52 `aoe`, 153 `single`, 76 `self`, 8 `summon`, 1 each `swarm`, `wish` and `grease`; 62 refused by the NPC path); a changed, added or removed spell is one failure naming it, so the suite's `passed` in the baseline is the spell count plus two. After a deliberate routing change (a new spell, a reclassification, batch 11's area casting for NPCs), regenerate the file from an edit-mode CommandScript with `result.Log(Tests.Magic.SpellRoutingGoldenTests.WriteGolden());`, update the baseline's `passed`, and list every changed spell in the commit body. The PC branch and NPC refusal are hand copies of those two methods' decision order, not calls into them (both methods carry a comment that points here; keep them in step): the suite catches routing data changes (reclassification, target type, shape, `SpellEffectRouting`), while a change to the routing code that leaves the data alone passes it and must be checked by the rules scenarios. Some rows encode known defects and are expected to change when those are fixed: `calm_animals`, `consecrate`, `desecrate`, `wall_of_thorns`, `persistent_image`, `chaos_hammer`, `holy_smite`, `orders_wrath` and `unholy_blight` were `Area` with no shape and routed as `single` for a PC until batch 11 step 23 (2026-10-16) gave them their areas (SPL-042, SPL-046); since then the PC branch follows `AoESystem.RoutesToArea` (an Area target type or a shape, the summons first) and the file has 292 spells: 63 `aoe`, 141 `single`, 77 `self`, 8 `summon`, 1 each `swarm`, `wish` and `grease`; 63 refused by the NPC path. Twelve rows changed in step 23: `bane` (now `Area` with a burst, `aoe`, refused by the NPC path, and Control: its new mind-affecting flag makes `SpellCategoryClassifier` file it so, as Crushing Despair) and `sound_burst` (now `Area` with a burst, `aoe`, refused by the NPC path), `chaos_hammer`, `holy_smite`, `unholy_blight`, `consecrate`, `desecrate` and `calm_animals` (burst, `aoe`), `orders_wrath`, `wall_of_thorns` and `persistent_image` (cube, `aoe`) and `passwall` (`Self`, `self`, no longer refused). Verified 2026-10-15: generated with 292 spells; 294/294 in edit and Play mode; with Acid Fog's golden effect type edited to Damage the suite failed with `spell routing changed: acid_fog` (REGRESSION), and passed again once the file was restored.

**Combat-log leak sweep.** Until 2026-10-08 every combat log call left about 50 inactive `PooledLogMsg` root objects behind (UI-001): a session reached 175,466 of them after 28 soak fights and fell below 1 frame per second, and the sweep destroyed 919,509 in the 50 soak fights below. `CombatLogPanel` now builds its line pool once and keeps free lines under an inactive `CombatLogPool` child, so no `PooledLogMsg` is ever a scene root. The pool creates a line only when it has no free one, so the panel holds at most `CombatLogPanel.MaxLineObjects` = 501 line objects (500 visible plus the one taken before the oldest is trimmed); the pool's configured cap of 550 free lines is never reached. The sweep stays as a safety net: every 30 frames, before the job is finalized and after its cleanup, `ScenarioRunner.SweepLogLeak` destroys any inactive `PooledLogMsg` root and checks the game's panel (`CombatLogPanel.CountLineObjects`): more than one `CombatLogPool` holder or more than 501 line objects is a leak that is not at the root, reported but not cleaned up. Whenever it finds a leak it reports it: a console warning on the job's first find, a `log-leak` trace event per find (destroyed, when, `atF`, `atR`, and `holders` and `lines` for a panel find, recorded again only when they grew; written after the `verdict` event and left out of the hash, so the trace hash is the same with or without a leak), a job note, a run-level entry in the summary's `warnings`, the warning count in the run's `index.jsonl` line and in the batch result (`ScenarioHarness.Poll()`, batch.json). The merged soak report does not carry them. Nothing in game state or the RNG changes. Verified 2026-10-08 by creating 3 stray roots during `rules/combat-log-pool` seed 1 (one `log-leak` event, the note and warning say 3, and the hash matched the clean run) and by removing the pool guard for one run (20261008-024908: the panel find said 1,051 holders and 52,550 lines, and index.jsonl and batch.json showed `warnings` 1). After the fix, one Play session ran 30 soak fights (seeds 11-40, runs 20261008-023102 and -023121, both OK, no `warnings`): the combat log's line objects (visible plus free) went 50, 100, 192 after 10 fights, 501 after 30 (they grow with the longest log of the session, up to 501), inactive `PooledLogMsg` roots stayed at 0 and the scene's root count at 28. Per-job frame rates (editor focus not controlled) ranged from 20 to 218 frames per second and were 41 to 129 over the last 10 fights; before the fix a session fell below 1 after 28 fights. Checked through object and child counts only: the on-screen log (rendering, scrolling, `ClearLog` when combat ends) is not verified in Play mode.

**The soak** (`Catalog/SoakScenarios.cs`, `soak/dmg-random-encounters`, tag `soak`). The four Quick Start characters (3rd level: Fighter, Rogue, Cleric, Wizard, all AI-run with class profiles, on the left side) fight a random dungeon encounter from the DMG tables (DMG p.78: choose the dungeon level, roll d% on its table, which can send the roll to the next lower or higher table, and roll the number appearing; DMG p.77: a party belongs where the dungeon level matches its level). The seed picks dungeon level 1 + (seed - 1) mod 3 and, through the seeded RNG, the roll (`DungeonEncounterTableManager.GenerateRandomEncounter`, then `DungeonEncounterSpawner.PrepareEncounter` for class levels and templates; an encounter whose creatures are all missing from `NPCDatabase` is rerolled up to 5 times; the per-job `spawn_...` ids are unregistered after the job). Creatures, at most `ScenarioLimits.MaxNpcActors` (40 since ENC-001), are placed on the right half (x 11-18, y 2-17) largest first with a free square between them; the game's `CustomEncounterBuilderUI.CalculateSpawnPositions` ignores creature size, so the soak places them itself. MaxRounds 30, wall cap 600 s per job (the definition's `.WallCap`), any outcome. A job passes when the fight ends in a Victory or Defeat the game declares itself, or in a Stalemate at the round cap, no unwaived invariant broke and nothing ended as an error. The only waiver left is the global CORE-003 one; the CORE-011 and CORE-037 waivers were deleted with their fix on 2026-10-08, so an undetected end is now a Fail.

Each soak job's result carries `soak` (outcome, rounds, turns and per team: actors, down at the end, turns, attacks (every weapon attack roll, AoOs included), hits, AoOs, maneuvers and successful maneuvers, casts begun on the NPC path, own moves and squares) and `generated` (encounter name, dungeon level, target EL, creatures). summary.json aggregates them as `soak` (outcomes, verdicts, mean rounds, turns and wall seconds, per team totals, per job and per turn, and one compact row per seed), and the run writes `soak-rows.tsv`. A batch merges the rows of its runs into `Logs/Scenarios/soak-<batchId>.json`; `ScenarioHarness.SoakReport("runId1,runId2,...")` merges any runs. Any run with a `soak`-tagged scenario writes these, `smoke/soak-mini` included, so a `rules/*,smoke/*` batch also leaves a `soak-<batchId>.json`.

Run it: `ScenarioHarness.QueueFresh("soak/*", "1-50", "perSession=10")` (about 3 minutes in five sessions on a freshly started editor: batch 20261009-165858 took about 2 min 50 s, of which the jobs are 135 s and the rest is the fresh Play sessions; a much longer batch, or first jobs that take a minute or more, means slow domain reloads: check the reload time in `Editor.log`, section 3.1 notes, TST-037 and TST-036). For the determinism check, make both calls in one CommandScript so they land in one batch: `QueueFresh("soak/*", "1-3", "repeat=2")` and `QueueFresh("soak/*", "1,2", "mode=watch")`; then compare the hashes with the fast run. (Made in separate commands, the second call is refused once Play mode has started, or starts a new batch once the first is idle: note the first batch's run ids before it.)

**AI-depth baseline of 2026-10-07** (commit c92a217 plus this change; batch 20261007-204349, runs 20261007-204356, -204505, -204529, -204829, -205104; merged in `soak-20261007-204349.json`). 50 jobs, every run verdict OK: 24 Pass, 26 Known, no Fail or Error. Outcomes: 8 Victory, 24 VictoryUndetected (CORE-011 18 times with `cause=hp<=0`, CORE-037 7 times with `cause=unconscious>0`), 4 Defeat (one declared while a cleric at 0 HP was still in the fight, `combat-end-early`, CORE-037), 1 DefeatUndetected, 13 Stalemate at the 30-round cap. Mean 13.2 rounds, 71.6 turns, 8.8 s per job (longest 42.6 s); 442 s of runs in all. By dungeon level (Victory / VictoryUndetected / Stalemate / Defeat or DefeatUndetected): level 1 3 / 10 / 4 / 0, level 2 2 / 7 / 5 / 3, level 3 3 / 7 / 4 / 2.

| Per turn | Attacks | Hits | AoOs | Maneuvers | Maneuver successes | Casts | Moves | Squares |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Party (2,244 turns) | 0.167 | 0.094 | 0.082 | 0.154 | 0.037 | 0.277 | 0.052 | 0.292 |

Found by the 2026-10-08 run below: seeds 25-30 and 37-40 of this baseline are contaminated by CRE-046 (fixed 2026-10-08; recheck results after the 2026-10-08 soak below) (their enemy fought with the Babble aura and incorporeality left on the slot by the allip of seed 24 or 36), so these figures include 10 contaminated fights.

Enemy casts are 0 because no encounter in seeds 1-50 rolled a spellcaster (the creatures are in the runs' `soak-rows.tsv`). Abilities that are not spells (the dretch's spell-like abilities, the lantern archon's light rays, hell hound breath) are not counted as casts: only `GameManager.TryNPCPerformSpellCast` raises the `SpellCast` hook.

What the baseline shows (read from the traces): the AI-run cleric cast a cure spell on itself at full HP on 468 of its 624 turns (AI-047); 103 grapple attempts (86 by the party, 17 by enemies) were refused because the target was already grappling someone, and each still used the attack (CMB-121); trips dominate the maneuvers on both sides and mostly fail (party 54 of 207, enemies 42 of 227); against a locust swarm the party could not hurt it and the swarm made AoOs (AI-059, CMB-123, SPL-121); two incorporeal undead (allip, seeds 24 and 36) spawned dead (CRE-044). Stalemates with a healthy party facing one or two weak enemies (seeds 5, 7, 34, 39) come from these wasted turns; seed 39's ogre also used Babble and had 50% concealment carried over from the seed 36 allip (CRE-046, fixed 2026-10-08; with the fix and the rules of 2026-10-08 seed 39 ends VictoryUndetected in 6 rounds), so that stalemate is not a clean AI reading.

Determinism: seeds 1-3 with `repeat=2` (run 20261007-194438) gave the same hash for each repetition; seeds 1-2 in `mode=watch` (run 20261007-205259, about 20 s each at real time) gave the same hashes as the fast run 20261007-204356; seeds 1-20 gave the same hashes in one long session (run 20261007-194724, interrupted after 20 jobs) as in fresh sessions; only seed 3 of the first run (20261007-194438) differs, because the `grid` invariant then still flagged the swarm sharing a square.

Against the 2026-10-07 baseline, enemies attack less per turn (0.396 against 0.499) and move fewer squares (0.165 against 0.362), and the party casts slightly more; the rules changes of the session (maneuvers in place of attacks, CMB-102 and AI-060; trip size limit and counter-trip, CMB-079; the 1-round pin, CMB-120) change the fights, so the two sets are not comparable job by job. 13 seeds changed outcome; the hydra (seeds 26 and 30) still beats the party in a fresh session too (runs 20261008-030430 and -030459: Defeat in 7 and 9 rounds).

Abnormal: CRE-046 (fixed 2026-10-08, see the recheck below). Enemy slot 0 kept the allip's incorporeality and Babble aura after seed 24 (and again after seed 36), so seeds 25-30 and 37-40 fought a creature that fascinates with Babble and has 50% concealment (the party spent turns on Listen checks), whatever the encounter rolled; seeds 26 and 30 run alone had neither. Those 10 jobs, in this run and in the 2026-10-07 baseline alike, depended on the seeds run before them in the session. The per-turn figures above still include them.

**Soak recheck after the CRE-046 fix** (2026-10-08, uncommitted tree on 24964f3). Seeds 21-40 in two batch sessions (runs 20261008-033320 and -033358, after `rules/*` jobs in the same Play session) and again in batch 20261008-034527 (runs 20261008-034804 and -034906, `perSession=10`, after the AI-profile destroy was added), and the seeds that had moved alone in fresh sessions (batch 20261008-033453, `perSession=1`: 25, 27, 28, 29, 34, 37, 38, 39, 40). Every run verdict OK, no `warnings`, no Babble line anywhere. Every seed gave the same trace hash in every run, and each seed that moved gave it alone in a fresh session too, so a seed's result no longer depends on the session split. Seeds 21-24 and 31-33, 35 and 36 kept the hashes of the 2026-10-08 soak, and seeds 26 and 30 now give the hashes those two seeds had alone in fresh sessions (runs 20261008-030430 and -030459). Results of the seeds that moved (old in-batch result in brackets): 25 VictoryUndetected in 6 rounds (VictoryUndetected in 27), 26 Defeat in 7 (Defeat in 9), 27 VictoryUndetected in 4 (in 21), 28 Victory in 1 (VictoryUndetected in 14), 29 Stalemate (Stalemate), 30 Defeat in 9, Known (Defeat in 8), 37 Victory in 1 (VictoryUndetected in 6), 38 Stalemate (Stalemate), 39 VictoryUndetected in 6 (Stalemate), 40 Victory in 5 (Stalemate). Seed 34 had no allip before it, yet its old hash differed too: an enemy that was dying from its first turn showed the move action as spent on every turn, an action-economy flag left on its pool slot by the creature of an earlier seed (dying creatures do not start a new turn), which the reset now clears; its outcome did not change. The full 50 seeds were not rerun then; the batch 3 soak below is that rerun.

- Seeds 14, 38 and others show different AoO triggers and grapple timing from the once-a-round movement AoO (CMB-128) and the shared-square reach (CMB-131).

- In seed 7 the skeleton ended Stable at -1.
- In seed 42 the zombie shambler ended Dying at -4.

The zombie's 0 HP state is older than CMB-136, which only made the zombie's counter-trip possible.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Totals: party 297 attacks, 147 AoOs, 340 maneuvers (87 succeeded), 164 casts, 445 moves; enemies 454 attacks, 163 AoOs, 303 maneuvers (61 succeeded), 0 casts (still no spellcaster rolled). Against batch 6 (commit d017ffb: 38 Victory, 7 Defeat, 5 Stalemate, mean 8.14 rounds; party 489 casts and 50 moves in 1,265 turns): only 7 seeds keep their trace hash (11, 12, 15, 24, 28, 35 and 36, all fights of one or two rounds), 18 keep their outcome and length, and 12 changed outcome: seeds 5, 7, 20, 26 and 32 became Victories (5 from a Stalemate, the others from Defeats), 8 and 30 became Defeats (from Victories), 3 and 50 became Stalemates (from Defeats), and 4, 19 and 40 became Stalemates (from Victories in 9, 5 and 4 rounds). The main cause, read from the traces, is the SPL-005 AI change (no cure on an unhurt creature): in batch 6 the AI-run cleric spent 348 of its 365 turns curing itself at full HP (AI-047), and now its 27 casts in 414 turns all cure a hurt creature. The turns it gained are not used. Counted on `turn_end` events (414; the traces hold 415 cleric `turn_start` events), the cleric moved on 387 turns (388 `move` events; 374 "moves to get a better shot" in 41 of the 50 seeds) and made no attack of its own (13 AoOs only). On 298 of those 387 turns (26 seeds) a party member was below the Healer's 70% threshold, so the turn was a Healing turn: the cure found no ally within touch range (the nearest such ally was 2 to 12 squares away, 3 or 4 on 205 turns), and the ranged-kiter fallback moved the cleric to 4 squares (`HealingRangeSquares`) from the enemy it picked, never toward the ally (AI-047). On 83 (38 seeds) every party member was at 75% or more, a Buffing turn, yet no buff was cast (Bless is an area spell, which the NPC cast path refuses, AI-001; Spiritual Weapon and the orisons Guidance, Light and Detect Magic were never cast either, why not was not traced), and the same move followed; 4 more were OffensiveSpell turns. The 27 casts were 12 Cure Light, 12 Cure Moderate and 3 Cure Minor Wounds. The clearest example is seed 19 (run 20261009-083038, Tiny vipers): the rogue is Disabled from round 7 and the wizard from round 13, both then Dying and Stable at -3 (from rounds 18 and 16), while the cleric, 5 squares from the rogue, steps between (11,1) and (12,1) from round 8 to the 30-round cap and cures neither. In seed 4 (run 20261009-082525) the cleric moves on every turn from round 1, steps between (6,5) and (7,5) from round 3 to round 18 and between (7,5) and (8,5) from round 21 to the cap, while the wizard lies at 5 of 10 HP and from round 16 Disabled; that Stalemate also comes from 23 refused grapples by the fighter and rogue against kobold e4, already in a grapple (CMB-121), which by then lies Stable at -9 with 85 nonlethal damage (AI-058), and from two kobolds at 4 HP that are never finished. The pacing is filed as AI-063 (Med), and AI-047 is raised to Med. The fewer Defeats probably follow from the cleric healing hurt allies (seeds 7 and 32 now end with no hero down, 20 and 26 with two), and the new Stalemates from a party that fights with three members (not traced seed by seed). The wizard's idle turns (148 of 389, 121 of 321 in batch 6) are as before. No invariant broke in any job and the static suites and rules scenarios show no regression; this is the new reference for later soaks.

## 4. Manual testing

### 4.1 Starting a session

Press Play in `Assets/Scenes/MainScene.unity`; `_Core/SceneBootstrap.cs` builds everything at runtime. On the character-creation screen (`UI/CharacterCreation/CharacterCreationUI.cs`):

- **Play Now!** (`OnVeryQuickStart`, line 2802) creates Fighter Aldric (Dwarf), Rogue Lyra (Elf), Cleric Theron (Human) and Wizard Elara (Elf) from each class's `GetQuickStartCharacter()`.
- **Quick Start** (`OnQuickStart`) picks a party size of 1-6 from the 11 PHB quick-start characters. Only PC1-PC4 exist and `GameManager.SetupCreatedCharacters` stops at 4, so sizes 5-6 probably drop the extra characters (not verified in Play mode).

Then the Encounter Selection UI opens (`GameManager.PromptEncounterSelection`, `_Core/GameManager.cs:785`), followed by the Pre-Combat Hub (`UI/Encounter/PreCombatHubUI.cs`: Manage Inventory (Stash), Open Store, Prepare Spells, Crafting Workshop, Start Encounter, Back to Encounter Selection).

### 4.2 Test encounter presets

24 presets in `NPCDatabase.ListEncounterPresets()` (`Character/Creatures/NPCDatabase.cs:90-117`) are test scenarios. `GameManager.ApplyEncounterPreset` (`_Core/GameManager.cs:1769`) matches the id against the constants at `GameManager.cs:100-121` and calls a `Configure<X>TestParty()` method in `_Core/GameManager.TestConfigs.cs`. Those methods re-`Init` PC1-PC4 with fixed stats and deactivate unused PCs. The replacement persists: picking a normal preset next only re-activates PCs (`RestoreStandardPartyLayout`), so restart Play mode to get your created party back (CORE-004 in [issues/CORE.md](issues/CORE.md)).

| Preset id | Active PCs | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `test_2_goblins`, `xp_levelup_test` | your party | No handler; standard layout. `xp_levelup_test` is one CR 15 "XP Pinata Goblin". |
| `grapple_test`, `feint_sneak_test`, `celestial_template_test`, `fiendish_template_test`, `ogre_battle_test`, `npc_magic_missile_test`, `disrupt_undead_test` | 1 | |
| `wizard_spell_test`, `true_strike_test`, `cleric_spell_test` | 1 | Level 20 caster (Wizard: Archmage Theron; Cleric: High Priestess Ilyra) with every implemented class spell auto-prepared, vs `target_dummy`. `true_strike_test` reuses the wizard setup. |
| `turn_undead_test`, `shield_bash_test`, `summon_monster_test` | 2 | |
| `armor_targeting_test`, `tiger_hunt_test` | 3 | |
| `grease_test` (2), `protection_from_evil_test` (1), `wind_dispersion_test` (2, displayed as "Obscuring Mist Test"), `obscuring_mist_ranged_only` (3), `charm_person_test` (2), `sleep_spell_test` (1), `mirror_image_test` (1) | as shown | **Spell preparation probably broken.** See below. |

The last row's presets set `PreparedSpellSlotIds` to level-1+ spells only (`GameManager.TestConfigs.cs:159, 1021, 1104, 1150, 1278, 1568, 1636, 1702`). `SpellcastingComponent.ApplyPreparedSpellSlotIds` (`Spell/Components/SpellcastingComponent.cs:1258`) fills slots by index, slots start with level 0, and `IsValidSpellForSlot` (:1446) requires an exact level match, so the entries are rejected and auto-prepare is skipped. Expect Console warnings `Cannot prepare level 1 spell in level 0 slot!` and `Ignored invalid prepared spell`. The fix is to pad with cantrips first, as `ConfigureNpcMagicMissileTestParty` does (:933). Until then, cast through the F12 panel or try the hub's Prepare Spells (not verified).

### 4.3 F12 Spell Testing Panel

`UI/Spells/SpellTestingPanel.cs` is added to the canvas unconditionally (`SceneBootstrap.cs:193`, no debug-build guard) and toggled with F12 (`Update`, line 92; key check at :94). It lists every `SpellDatabase` spell (placeholders shown with a darker background), filters by text and level, offers metamagic toggles, and keeps damage/save/SR counters.

`CastSpell` (line 1096) works only during a PC's turn (`gm.ActivePC` must be non-null; otherwise it logs "No active PC!"). Bypasses applied to that PC, so do not treat results as normal play:

- A non-caster temporarily gets a Wizard 10 class level and a `SpellcastingComponent` (`EnsureTemporaryCasterSetup`).
- +4 to the casting ability (`ApplyAbilityBoost`); `Update` restores it (and the temporary Wizard setup) once `GetTestPanelCaster()` returns null.
- `EnsureSpellAvailable` adds the spell to known and prepared lists regardless of class list or caster level, adds a slot at the spell's level if none is ready, sets `SlotsRemaining` to 99 and un-uses spent slots holding that spell.
- `GameManager.TestCastSpellFromPanel` (`_Core/GameManager.TestPanel.cs:43`) forces `CurrentPhase = PCTurn`, opens the cast's own context (`BeginCast` with a clone of the template, the metamagic applied, and a `TestPanel` `CastSource` whose `TestPanelBypass` flags are `Components` and `ArcaneFailure`, plus `Slots` for infinite slots; batch 11 step 4), and calls `BeginPendingSpellTargeting`. `OnCellClicked` falls back to `GetTestPanelCaster()` (`GameManager.CombatActions.cs:558`). Because `ActivePC` already requires `PCTurn` (`GameManager.cs:282-291`), forcing the phase has no effect in practice, and the class comment's claim that the panel works outside a PC turn is outdated (UI-021, UI-046 in [issues/UI.md](issues/UI.md)).
- For the whole cast, three checks are skipped: the arcane spell failure roll (`GameManager.TryRollArcaneSpellFailure`, the `ArcaneFailure` flag), the component test of the cast core's Legality stage and the spend of priced material components (the `Components` flag; `SpellComponentRules.IsExempt`, one rule for every caster since batch 11 step 17: the pouch, the divine focus, priced foci and material components, Stoneskin's diamond dust included). Since batch 11 step 4 (2026-10-15) the flags are the cast's own: they hold through a single-target, touch or self cast's resolution as well as an area, summon, Grease or held-touch cast (every path makes the component test since batch 11 step 15, and every caster since step 17), and they end with the cast, which also forgets the panel's caster, so nothing leaks into the next cast or an NPC's (SPL-036, fixed; checked by `rules/stale-cast-state-f12-bypass`). `infiniteSlots` sets the `Slots` flag, which nothing reads (UI-046).

### 4.4 Other dev hooks

Left Ctrl+H cycles the HP calculation mode (`Utilities/DebugCommands.cs`). `Identifiers/EnumTest.cs` and `IdentifierTest.cs` are editor-only `[ContextMenu]` smoke checks, not attached anywhere. There are no other test scripts: the root Python scripts `phase5_validation.py` and `test_range_calculator.py`, which tested Python re-implementations rather than the C# code, were deleted (read them from git history if needed).

## 5. Manual scenarios

### 5.1 Aid Another

Code: `Combat/Maneuvers/SupportActions.cs` (a `partial class GameManager`; the file's `SupportActions` class itself is empty). No preset exists; use a multi-PC party (Play Now! + `test_2_goblins`) and move characters into the layouts below (read "Orc" as any enemy). Distances are Chebyshev squares, reach-aware via `CharacterController.ThreatensWith`. Equipping a longspear for case 2 was not checked; the Store or stash may have one.

Flow:

0. Open **Special Attack** and choose **Aid Another**. The main-panel Aid Another button is always hidden (`UI/Combat/ActionButtonPanel.cs:706-707`). The entry (`UI/Combat/CombatUI.cs:1171-1174`) reads `Aid Another (Standard)` or `Aid Another (<reason>)` with reason `Used`, `Pinned`, `No ally` or `No target`, and is disabled unless `CanUseAidAnother` passes. `OnAidAnotherButtonPressed` has a pinned-to-grapple-menu redirect, but it is not reached from this menu because `CanUseAidAnother` already rejects pinned characters.
1. If both an aid target and an adjacent sleeping ally exist, a picker offers **Aid Attack/Defense** or **Wake Sleeping Ally**; otherwise this step is skipped.
2. Select an enemy the initiator threatens with its equipped weapon.
3. Choose **Aid Defense (+2 AC)** or **Aid Offense (+2 Attack)**.
4. Select an ally who threatens that enemy with the ally's own weapon. Reach-weapon allies adjacent to the enemy do not qualify; unarmed or ranged-weapon allies count as threatening adjacent squares.
5. The log shows `Melee touch attack vs AC 10:` with roll, modifier (BAB + STR + size + condition penalty) and total; on a miss, `Aid Another failed (needed 10).` The standard action is spent either way.

| Case | Layout | Expected |
|---|---|---|
| 1 Adjacent | Fighter (6,5) longsword, Rogue (6,6), Orc (7,6) | Orc listed; choose Defense; Rogue listed. With Fighter at (5,5) instead, the menu shows `Aid Another (No target)`. |
| 2 Reach | Fighter (5,5) longspear, Rogue (7,6), Orc (7,5) | Orc listed (distance 2); choose Offense; Rogue listed. |
| 3 No ally in range | Fighter (5,5), Rogue (10,10), Orc (6,5) | After choosing the aid type: `No allies in melee range of Orc!`; back to the action menu, no action spent. |
| 4 Several enemies | Fighter (5,5), Orc1 (6,5), Orc2 (5,6), Goblin (10,10), Rogue (7,5) | Orc1 and Orc2 listed, not Goblin. Select Orc1: Rogue listed. |
| 5 Several allies | Fighter (5,5), Rogue (7,5), Wizard (6,6), Cleric (10,10), Orc (6,5) | Rogue and Wizard listed, not Cleric. |

Bonus checks: each successful aider adds a +2 entry and entries stack; the first matching attack consumes them (offense: beneficiary attacks that enemy; defense: that enemy attacks the beneficiary). Unused entries expire at the beneficiary's second turn start. The Character Sheet shows an "AID ANOTHER BONUSES" section only while bonuses are active (`CharacterSheetUI.AppendAidAnotherSection`). The AI never uses Aid Another: `AIBehaviorData.UseAidAnother` exists but nothing reads it.

### 5.2 Sleep

Code: `GameManager.ResolveSleepSpell` (`Spell/Resolution/GameManager.SpellCasting.cs:5131`). Automated coverage: `Tests.Combat.SleepSpellRulesTests.RunAll()` (definition, Asleep condition, wake helper, Aid Another availability; not the HD pool).

Setup: `sleep_spell_test` makes PC1 Archmage Theron (Wizard 20, only active PC) against skeleton_archer (1 HD undead, immune), goblin_warchief (2 HD), orc_berserker (3 HD) and human_cleric (5 HD, over the cap). Its prepared list fails (section 4.2), so cast Sleep from the F12 panel on Theron's turn. Immune and over-cap creatures are dropped silently; the `Candidates: N` count excludes them.

1. **HD pool.** Cast so the 10-ft burst covers all four enemies. Log: `HD Pool: N (4d4) | Duration: R rounds | Will DC D` and `Result: K target(s) asleep. Remaining HD pool: P.` Repeat via F12 (slots refresh); N stays within 4-16.
2. **Ordering and cap.** Lowest HD first; ties broken by distance from the caster, not the burst center. A target larger than the remaining pool logs `(X HD) exceeds remaining pool (P) — skipped.` HD are deducted before SR and the Will save, so a resisting target still uses pool. human_cleric is never affected.
3. **Wake on damage.** Damage a sleeper (lethal or nonlethal): `<name> wakes from taking damage.`; Asleep is removed.
4. **Wake Sleeping Ally.** Not possible in `sleep_spell_test` (single PC). Use Play Now! + `test_2_goblins`: Elara casts Sleep (F12) on a burst that includes Aldric or the cleric Theron (Sleep affects allies; elves, including Elara and Lyra, are immune; the ally needs 4 HD or less and gets a Will save, so retry if it resists). An adjacent PC uses Special Attack, Aid Another, then Wake Sleeping Ally. Expect a standard action spent, `<aider> shakes <ally> awake.`, and Asleep plus Unconscious removed.
5. **Duration.** 1 min/level = 10 rounds per caster level (200 rounds for Theron). Use a low-level caster and end turns until `⏱ <name> wakes as sleep duration expires.`
